- **semantic-git**: Generate and maintain human-readable documentation that explains what a codebase does, so someone can understand the system without reading code. Use this skill whenever the user commits code, opens a PR, asks to document a repo, asks "what does this code do", wants an overview of a codebase, mentions generating docs, says "explain this repo", says "document this", or runs /semantic-git. Also trigger after any commit or PR creation to keep docs up to date. If the user says anything about understanding, explaining, or documenting code, use this skill. ([kwosei/semantic-git](https://github.com/kwosei/semantic-git/tree/HEAD/skills/semantic-git))

([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-supervised-cell-type-annotation))
- **semi-supervised-cell-type-annotation-via-gating**: Use when integrating heterogeneous scRNA-seq samples (e.g., TIL collections across tumor types) where only partial or noisy cell-type labels exist and you want gating-derived priors to drive semi-supervised integration. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-supervised-cell-type-annotation-via-gating))
- **semi-supervised-em-mixture-model-clustering**: Use when filtering droplet-based snRNA-seq data where hard count cutoffs or EmptyDrops fail to remove ambient/debris RNA, and you need expression-profile-based clustering that fixes low-count droplets as debris before fitting a mixture model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-supervised-em-mixture-model-clustering))
- **semi-supervised-gene-weighting-clustering**: Use when analyzing paired control/perturbed scRNA-seq data (e.g. gene knock-outs, treatments) where the goal is to identify cell states unique to the perturbed condition without a priori knowledge of marker genes or cell states. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-supervised-gene-weighting-clustering))
- **semi-supervised-integration-with-cell-type-labels**: Use when integrating multiple scRNA-seq datasets where partial or imperfect cell type labels exist for at least a reference subset, and preserving cell-type biology (e.g., CD8 T cell subtypes) while removing batch effects matters. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-supervised-integration-with-cell-type-labels))
- **semi-supervised-label-propagation**: Use when single-cell RNA-seq cells already have partially confident labels from a consensus of annotation tools (e.g. scPred, SingleR, ScType, SCINA, scSorter) and you need to propagate those labels to unconfident cells with a graph convolutional network. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-supervised-label-propagation))
- **semi-supervised-learning**: Use when applying semi-supervised learning techniques. ([LoopyLuci/Skills](https://github.com/LoopyLuci/Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/semi-supervised-learning) · ★2)
- **semi-supervised-leiden-clustering-with-frozen-clusters**: Use when refining label-transfer annotations of a query scRNA-seq dataset (e.g., HBMP2-spleen-2 against an annotated HBMP3-spleen-CC3 reference) where unconstrained Leiden clustering alone disagrees with reference labels and a semi-automatic, expert-guided correction is acceptable. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-supervised-leiden-clustering-with-frozen-clusters))
- **semi-supervised-louvain-clustering**: Use when clustering a perturbation scRNA-seq AnnData where control cells already have trusted cluster labels (e.g. from Louvain on controls) and treated cells must be assigned without letting them distort the control-defined states. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-supervised-louvain-clustering))
- **semi-supervised-novelty-detection-eval**: Evaluates a model's ability to distinguish in-distribution (ID) samples from out-of-distribution (OOD) samples in a semi-supervised setting, where only labeled ID data and unlabeled mixed data are available during training. Use when the user wants to benchmark on MNIST, FashionMNIST, SVHN, CIFAR10, CIFAR100, ImageNet, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports AUROC. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/semi-supervised-novelty-detection-eval) · ★3)
- **semi-supervised-scissors-reclustering**: Use when a scRNA-seq clustering (e.g., Seurat Louvain) yields broad clusters suspected of containing rare or low-abundance subtypes that standard resolution sweeps miss. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-supervised-scissors-reclustering))
- **semi-supervised-scrna-seq-integration**: Use when integrating multiple scRNA-seq batches where partial or imperfect cell type labels are available and preserving cell-type biological variance (e.g., celltype ASW, CiLISI) is a priority alongside batch mixing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-supervised-scrna-seq-integration))
- **semi-supervised-svm-rescoring**: Use when an unfiltered search-engine PSM list (e.g. XTandem, MaxQuant, mzid, tsv) is available as a feature table with target/decoy labels and the goal is to increase peptide identifications at a fixed FDR or tighten the FDR threshold. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/semi-supervised-svm-rescoring))
- **semi-synthetic-benchmark-evaluation**: Use when comparing signal-removal methods on a semi-synthetic single-cell dataset where a known nuisance signal (e.g., cell cycle) and a ground-truth biological attribute (e.g., subclone labels) are both annotated, so removal and conservation can be scored quantitatively. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-synthetic-benchmark-evaluation))
- **semi-synthetic-benchmark-fdr-power-analysis**: Use when evaluating whether a variable-selection or conditional-dependency method controls FDR while gaining power on semi-synthetic single-cell data, especially when comparing hierarchical (group-level) vs. gene-level test resolutions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/semi-synthetic-benchmark-fdr-power-analysis))
- **semi-synthetic-label-perturbation**: Use when auditing a cell-annotation error detector: you need a ground-truth correct/incorrect flag per cell to compute AUROC, so labels must be synthetically misclassified at controlled fractions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/semi-synthetic-label-perturbation))
- **semi-synthetic-multi-omics-data-simulation**: Use when benchmarking multi-omics integration or pathway methods on real data that lacks validated molecular/pathway-level ground truth, by injecting a known enriched-pathway signal into real abundance matrices at controlled effect sizes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/semi-synthetic-multi-omics-data-simulation))
- **semi-synthetic-multi-omics-signal-injection**: Use when no real benchmarking dataset with validated molecular- and pathway-level signals exists, and you need controlled multi-omics ground truth across effect sizes to compare molecular-level vs pathway-level predictive models. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/semi-synthetic-multi-omics-signal-injection))
- **semi-synthetic-response-construction-transcriptomic**: Use when benchmarking high-dimensional regression methods (e.g. SuffPCR vs sparse linear models vs non-sparse PCR) on real omics data where no true response exists, and ground-truth feature selection is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/semi-synthetic-response-construction-transcriptomic))
- **semi-synthetic-single-cell-data-simulation**: Use when you need ground-truth conditional dependencies to benchmark FDR control and power of feature-selection methods on single-cell data, or to compare importance-score choices (e.g., linear OLS vs MLP) via held-out predictive performance.
